> The owner asked to understand the 7,21 impossibility in analogous relation to all the recent work.
>
> ## What the 7,21 impossibility is
> `H(T) = I(Omega, 2)` (the Hamiltonian-path count = the independence polynomial of the odd-cycle conflict
> graph `Omega`, evaluated at 2) is, by Redei, ALWAYS ODD. It takes every odd value EXCEPT **{7, 21}** -- the
> only two gaps (verified by enumeration `n<=6`: achievable odds `1,3,5,9,11,13,15,17,19,23,25,...`, missing
> only `7, 21`). The H-spectrum is a **co-finite multiplicative numerical semigroup of genus 2**, gaps
> `{7,21} = {Phi_3(2), 3*Phi_3(2)}` (s699). MECHANISM (why-seven-is-forbidden): `H=7 = 1 + 2*3 + 4*0` means
> "three pairwise-conflicting odd cycles, zero independent depth" -- pure 3-fold curvature. In a TOURNAMENT
> (a COMPLETE digraph) three 3-cycles sharing a vertex span 5 vertices, and completeness forces a 5th cycle
> (a 5-cycle) -> contamination -> `H>7`. So `H=7` (and `21=3*7`) is impossible: **completeness cannot support
> isolated pure curvature.** `7 = |Fano PG(2,2)|`.

> | 5 | `+1/30` | spherical | icosahedron `{3,5}` |

> *kind-pasteur-2026-06-30. The owner asked to "use w as a log-barrier smoothing inside a Beurling–Selberg / Fourier-positivity signed certificate," and to look back at where the project has needed signed vs. unsigned series. This reflection assembles a signed-vs-unsigned survey, three fresh computations, and the existing canon (THM-515 Riesz product, THM-563 single-far periodicity, HYP-3130 Beurling–Selberg minorant, HYP-3132 multi-far floor) into one organizing frame: **the far speed is a self-concordant barrier, and peeling far runners follows a central path to a bounded-core analytic center.***
>
> > **Concurrent convergence (added at close-out, 2026-06-30).** opus **HYP-3769** independently reached the *same* interior-point frame — on the **covering-min `M`**, not the sector `p0`: the exact split `1/M = (n−1) + 1/rung` reads `ν = n−1` (the danger-arc count) as the self-concordant barrier parameter and the **tight AP as the analytic center** (rung 1), coverings pushed off it (rung ≥ 2). opus pushed first and **owns the covering-min framing**; this is the independent sector-`p0` version (`Plat(C)` = analytic center, `1/w` = barrier coordinate). Two functionals, one frame — mutual corroboration, not priority. opus also works with the log-barrier `F=−∫log m_w` directly (self-concordant), consistent with the correction (below) that the *tent* form is not.
>
> ## The one law the survey found
>
> Across every signed/unsigned instance in the repo — the `(−1)^{|T|}` singular series (THM-504, `A_3=∞`), the signed SPEC sum (HYP-3129), the Dedekind sawtooth (THM-563), the Legendre/Gauss `i√7` (the three modes), Pfaffian-vs-permanent (THM-515 §D), Euler's pentagonal signs — the same law holds:
>
> > **The hard, open content is always the signed / odd / imaginary part; positivity methods (Mayer gas, Schur envelope, absolute lattice bounds, SOS) reach only the even / real / positive part, and they diverge or overcount ~100× on the signed part.**
>
> The real side (`ℚ(cos 2π/7)`, equioscillation, cap, binding pairs) is closed. The imaginary side (`ℚ(√−7)`, the Gauss sum `i√7`, the decorrelation floor `R′`, the signed SPEC) is the open frontier. Any certificate must **keep positivity while extracting a signed cancellation** — which is exactly what a Riesz product does (THM-515), and exactly where the uniform Beurling–Selberg minorant fails: HYP-3130's `C^∞` minorant *closes the apex block* `r≤6` but **degrades the coupling ratio**, so it cannot be used uniformly to bridge the far–core coupling.

> *mac-mini-2026-06-29-S26. The owner asked to use the recent ideas — the obstruction lens, relations-not-things, the moment method, Γ₀(N) — to reframe the remaining LRC targets. They collapse.*
>
> ## Five obligations were always two
>
> For a long time the LRC(14) proof has carried a list. There is the witness density (THM-527, "the linchpin"): show `G2 > 0` forces `M ≥ 1/14`. There is the witness floor for `k = 8..13`. There is the gK8 concentration, `max_E ≤ 10·cap`. There is the doublet R-tail, `R(M) = O(1/M)`. And over all of them the gatekeeper: the covering floor `R' > 0`, equivalently `CV(N_R)^2` bounded. Five analytic obligations, each with its own machinery, each re-proved per configuration.
>
> Seen through the last six sessions, they are two — and the two are not new obligations but two *properties of a single object*. The object is the danger relation `D(v,t) = [\lVert vt\rVert < 1/14]` composed with itself, `D \cdot D^{\top}`. That composition is simultaneously the second moment, the pair-correlation that klein's THM-588 proved is the *only* invariant, and the R-equivariant obstruction class whose measure (S23) is the floor. Everything left to prove is two facts about it:
>
> > `D \cdot D^{\top}` is **essential** (it does not factor) and **bounded** (`CV(N_R)^2` is finite), under the right change of base.
>
> Essential gives existence — a lonely point. Bounded gives uniformity — the gatekeeper. They are the σ-odd and σ-even halves, the two measures of one obstruction.

## P030

Source: `.scratch/thm2754_lf_checkout/07-reflections/we-were-rehearsing-the-bulk-the-proof-is-at-the-cusp.md`, line 3. Blob: `51e65a67bdf8bb6628ebf8d0881c528b3848d26f`.

> *mac-mini-2026-06-29-S29. The owner asked what we are missing, given all the connections. The connections, read together, point at a place none of our rehearsals go.*
>
> ## The thing klein said and we kept not hearing
>
> In S4, klein wrote a sentence and moved on, and we all built on everything around it except the sentence itself: *the testbed models the bounded regime, not the `m_R → 0` corner — the binding worst-case.* For five sessions we refined the rehearsal — the metagraph as a finite Siegel transform, `CV(H)^2 ~ 2/n` proved clean under the transitive `S_n`, the `Z_7` cyclotomic Gram, the octonion-optimal flat spectrum. Every one of those is a statement about the *interior* of the moduli space, where things are generic and the symmetry is full. And the proof is not there. `CV(N_R)^2` is bounded in the bulk and blows up as `m_R → 0`; the floor binds exactly where our model stops modelling.
>
> So the question "what are we missing" has a precise answer: **a model of the cusp.** We have rehearsed the bulk to a theorem and have nothing for the boundary, and the boundary is the whole game.
>
> ## The cusps are X₀(14)'s, and there are four
>
> Once you name it as the cusp, the modular dictionary tells you exactly what it is. `14 = 2·7`, and the degenerate regimes where `m_R → 0` are the cusps of `X_0(14)`. The cusp count is `\sum_{d\mid N}\varphi(\gcd(d,N/d))`, which for `14` is **four**, indexed by the divisors `1, 2, 7, 14`. The cusp at `\infty` (`d=1`) is the bulk, where the metagraph lives. The cusp at `0` (`d=14`) is the fully-dense limit. The `d=2` cusp is the doubling. And the `d=7` cusp is the **apex** — `\text{speed-}7`, where `7a/14 = a/2` makes the apex prime correlate the even and odd sheets, the `2`-`7` coupling that S259 worried about and klein-S4's `\sup CV = 8.74` binds at. >         Owner asked me to work the representation theory of binary forms vs tournaments and pin down what in/transitivity itself is. This closes a loop between the S74 binary-forms consultation and kp's THM-1750 char-poly/trace template.
>
> THE MAP. Send a tournament (adjacency A) to the CHARACTERISTIC POLYNOMIAL of A -- a monic degree-n polynomial = a BINARY FORM phi_T in Sym^n P^1. Roots = eigenvalues; coefficients = power sums tr(A^k) via Newton's identities.
>
> TRANSITIVITY ITSELF -- five equivalent guises, one geometric point (verified n=3,4,5):
>   T transitive  <=>  phi_T = X^n  <=>  A nilpotent  <=>  tr(A^k)=0 for all k (kp THM-1750)
>      <=>  phi_T = l^n = the VERTEX of the rational normal curve C_n = {l^n} in Sym^n P^1
>      <=>  the DEEPEST point of the SL_2-GIT nullcone (root multiplicity n > n/2, maximally unstable).
> Because a tournament forces tr(A)=0, the only eigenvalue an l^n-tournament can have is 0, so the transitive tournament is the UNIQUE tournament lying on the rational normal curve. KEY REFRAME: transitivity is not a negative ('no cycles') -- it is a maximally DEGENERATE positive object, the cusp l^n, the origin of the moment map, the most unstable binary form. It is to tournaments what l^n is to forms and what the nilpotent-cone vertex is to matrices.
>
> INTRANSITIVITY ITSELF -- the deviation from l^n, graded by cycle length. p_1=p_2=0 always (no loops; A_ij A_ji=0), and the first nonzero power sum is tr(A^3)=3*(#directed 3-cycles) = the x^{n-3} coefficient of phi_T. So the 3-CYCLE COUNT is the LEADING coefficient of intransitivity -- the first SL_2-covariant that 'sees' a cycle; higher tr(A^k) give the full spectral shape. Intransitivity = distance from the rational normal curve, filtered by cycle length.

A and B are independent, so expanding U = A(1-Ab)+B binomially factorises the expectation; both factors are one-line Wick evaluations, E_A[A^k(1-Ab)^k] = (-1)^k k! since only the Ab^k term survives, and E_B[(1+Bb)^m B^{m-k}] = C(m,k)(m-k)!. The collapse C(m,k)^2 k!(m-k)! = m! C(m,k) then gives E[P^m] = m! (1-1)^m = 0 and E[QP^m] = m!(1-(1-1)^m) = m!. THE WHOLE COUNTEREXAMPLE IS THE BINOMIAL THEOREM -- one computation yields both lines, and the Q-insertion merely shifts the binomial index by one, deleting exactly the k = -1 term whose presence made the alternating sum vanish. > **Owner directive:** "aim to finish GMC(2) by finishing the stronger 2 dimensional nullcone conjecture."
>
> - **THE CONJECTURE.** `N(E) := {P : E[P^m]=0 for all m>=1}`. **NULLCONE CONJECTURE (n=2): N(E) = {all charges >= 1} u {all charges <= -1}**, i.e. exactly the STRICTLY ONE-SIDED charge support.
> - **WHY IT IS THE BETTER TARGET, and this is the methodological point.** A direct GMC(2) search has NO POSSIBLE POSITIVE CONTROL -- a positive control would BE the counterexample sought (the flaw I flagged in S135 and refused to lean on). The nullcone conjecture makes a POSITIVE PREDICTION -- one-sided P ARE in the nullcone -- so the machinery can be VALIDATED before its negatives are trusted. **All five controls pass.** That converts an uncontrolled sweep into meaningful evidence.
> - **(A) THE EXACT POLAR REDUCTION.** With `H_r(u) := P(ru, r/u)` -- a Laurent polynomial in u whose EXPONENTS ARE EXACTLY THE CHARGES and whose r-degree is the total degree -- **`E[P^m] = L( v -> CT_u( H_sqrt(v)(u)^m ) )`**, verified exactly. So CT_u is the charge-0 projection and L is the Gaussian average over the radius: **GMC(2) IS DUISTERMAAT-VAN DER KALLEN's CONSTANT-TERM PROBLEM, GAUSSIAN-AVERAGED.** That names the right context -- DvdK is the model theorem of the Mathieu-Zhao area.
> - **(B) THE TWO-CHARGE THEOREM -- PROVED.** For `P = P_C + P_{-B}` with exactly two charges C > 0 > -B: charge-0 forces `k_C = tB', k_{-B} = tC'` with `m = t(B'+C')`, UNIQUE when it exists and impossible otherwise. So **E[P^m] = 0 unless (B'+C')|m, and otherwise = C(m; tB', tC') * L(F^t) with F = v^{CB'} q^{B'} s^{C'} != 0**, which is nonzero for large t by THM-1520's saddle lemma. **Two-charge P is NEVER in the nullcone.** Verified on 6 explicit P -- nonzero exactly at the predicted multiples of B'+C'.
> - **(C) THE GENERAL REDUCTION.** `E[P^m] = sum_d [charge-0, degree-d coeff]*(d/2)!`, and the factorials weight the top degree overwhelmingly, so the leading term comes from the **TOP EDGE of the (charge, degree) Newton polygon where it crosses charge 0** -- and a single edge's monomials form a ONE-VARIABLE LAURENT polynomial. Needs (i) the 1-variable Laurent nullcone lemma (CT(h^m)=0 for all m => h one-sided; 10320 two-sided h tested, no exceptions) and (ii) DOMINATION of the leading term.
> - **(D) EVIDENCE, NOW CONTROLLED.** 400 / 10000 / 118800 / **886800** two-sided P at support sizes 2/3/4/5: **ZERO in the nullcone**, with the positive control passing.
> - **(E) GMC(2) IS A TWO-LINE COROLLARY.** P in N(E) => strictly one-sided => charges of P^m are >= m => QP^m has charge 0 only if m <= deg_W(Q) =>

## P093

Source: `00-navigation/SESSION-LOG.md`, line 3142. Blob: `76f72accb0b2674c75529a9ae7ee201209062a90`.

> **Owner directive:** still think nullcone; for inspiration, consider how {-1,0,1} and {1,1/2,0} are functionally equivalent but have each shown up in this repo many times -- this is what I mean by even/odd vs positive/negative.
>
> [A] THE DICTIONARY. x -> (1+x)/2 carries {-1,0,1} to {0,1/2,1}, and for tournaments this IS the standard A = (J - I + S)/2 between the SKEW adjacency S (entries +-1, zero diagonal) and the 0/1 adjacency A. Verified entrywise at n = 4,5,6, and the diagonal S_ii = 0 is exactly the 1/2. So the repo's two halves are one object in two coordinate systems: the FRACTION world {0,1/2,1} carries tilings, switching classes, cut+cycle, even graphs and two-graphs (THM-1430/1470); the SIGN world {-1,0,1} carries the skew adjacency, the Pfaffian, det S (THM-1475), the charge grading and the nullcone. The dictionary costs a 1/2, and 1/2 does not exist in characteristic 2. THAT is the even/odd versus positive/negative split, stated precisely.
>
> [B] THE COLLAPSE PRINCIPLE -- one line, and it retroactively explains my own work. Every off-diagonal entry of S is +-1, and +1 == -1 mod 2, so S == J - I (mod 2) FOR EVERY TOURNAMENT: mod 2 the skew adjacency forgets the orientation completely. THEOREM: if I(T) = F(S) for a polynomial F with INTEGER coefficients, then I(T) == F(J-I) mod 2 is the same value for every tournament on n vertices. COROLLARY: Pf(S) == Pf(J-I) = #{perfect matchings of K_n} = (n-1)!!, odd. So THM-1475 section 1, which I wrote up as a fact about Pfaffians, is just this principle at F = Pf. I did not see that when I proved it, and it is the kind of thing the dictionary makes obvious.
>
> [C] THE CENSUS confirms the split exactly (exhaustive at n = 4,5; 4096 tournaments at n = 6). CONSTANT mod 2: Pf(S), det(S), tr(S^2), tr(S^4) -- all integer polynomials in S. VARYING mod 2: c_3(T) and tr(A^3) -- whose expressions in S carry a 1/2. The invariants that can SEE the tournament mod 2 are precisely those whose definition passes through the halving.
>
> [D] THE REDEI CLASS IS SMALL, and this is the finding I did not expect. hp needs the 1/2 (it is a 0/1-world count) and yet is mod-2 blind -- that is Redei's theorem. Is it an instance of something structural? NO. Of five natural fraction-world counts tested at n = 4,5,6, ONLY hp is blind: Hamiltonian cycles, out-arborescences at a fixed root, transitive triples and 4-cycles all take both parities. Being a fraction-world count is not enough. (Bounded census: it shows these four companions fail, it does not prove hp unique.)
>
> [E] THE ASYMMETRY IS THE CONTENT, and it is the useful form of the owner's

## P096

Source: `00-navigation/SESSION-LOG.md`, line 594. Blob: `76f72accb0b2674c75529a9ae7ee201209062a90`.

> **Owner directive:** work LRC and moment nullcone.
>
> THE CONNECTION. Both are moment-nullcone problems (kind-pasteur THM-1750): a moment functional F, a nullcone where F collapses, a DETECTION DEPTH = how many moments certify. And both are, in death-star-S67's phrase, positivity of that functional past a cancellation wall. LRC(14)-covering: M(S) >= 1/14 <=> exists t with danger count X(t) = #{v: ||vt||<1/14} = 0, X in {0..13}; the certificate is the Bonferroni inclusion-exclusion B_m = sum_{k<=m}(-1)^k S_k on the factorial moments S_k = E[C(X,k)]. GMC(2): E[P^m]=0 all m => P one-sided, functional E[P^m] = L_s(CT_u[Lambda_s^m]).
>
> THE ONE DIFFERENCE -- a bounded vs unbounded alphabet. LRC's danger count is BOUNDED (X <= 13), so the factorial moments vanish for k>13, the Bonferroni TERMINATES, and a finite-depth certificate exists. The actual covering certificate is B5 (depth 5, my THM-671): the depth where the SIGNED (fermionic) Bonferroni truncation first stays positive against the worst CORRELATED ADVERSARIAL instance -- the cubic B3 goes negative on the C1uC4-killers and the 7-structured @91 cluster. GMC's alphabet is the radial degree -- UNBOUNDED. The moment engine is the permanent/hafnian functional (THM-1810): no sign, no cancellation, no termination, so the detection depth GROWS (>= d+1, EMP floor, THM-1790). SAME WALL, OPPOSITE FINITENESS, ONE STRUCTURAL REASON: boundedness of the moment alphabet. This is the precise content behind death-star-S67's reflection and explains the asymmetry the fleet has felt -- why LRC(14)-covering yields to a fixed quintic certificate while GMC(2) resists every finite-degree elimination (THM-1770).
>
> THE BOSONIC/FERMIONIC READING (THM-1810) on both: LRC's Bonferroni sum(-1)^k S_k is the fermionic/alternating truncation, the wall is where it dips negative, and B5 is the first positive (bosonic) truncation past it -- reachable at fixed depth BECAUSE the alphabet is finite. GMC's engine is purely bosonic (permanent, no sign) with infinite alphabet, so no terminating alternation and no fixed positive depth. LRC gets a FINITE FERMIONIC certificate; GMC is stuck with an INFINITE BOSONIC one.
>
> SELF-CORRECTION, recorded: my script first labelled the IID Bonferroni's positive-turn (depth 2) as 'the LRC detection depth'. That is the iid MEAN, which is too optimistic (no wall in the mean); the real correlated/adversarial LRC covering depth is 5 (B5, THM-671). The load-bearing point -- finite (5, <=13) for LRC vs unbounded for GMC -- is unchanged and correct.
>
> CONSEQUENCE FOR BOTH PROGRAMS: any attempt to give GMC(2) a fixed-depth

> ## 2026-09-27 pricing note (opus S15, fifth note): a direction misstated the residual obstruction
>
> - What was done: direction D14 of `collatz_pricing_approaches_20260927.md` said that the points of `E_inf` that are not negative integers are 2-adically approachable by small integers, so that generic growth words carry no size price.
> - Why it was wrong: for any fixed 2-adic point `x` the least positive integer in `x + 2^K Z` is `x mod 2^K`, of size `2^(K - z_K(x))` with `z_K` the run of zero bits of `x` just below position `K`; for the Sturmian point and for fixed points of random no-descent words `z_K` is a geometric fluctuation (computed in the sixth note), so generic points are priced by size up to `O(1)` bits exactly like the cycle points. A small integer `m` approaches deeply only points `x = m mod 2^K`, i.e. points that look like `m`.
> - Correct framing: the size price `K - z_K(x)` is universal; cheap growth is the least-representative phenomenon (an integer below `2^K` following a no-descent word of precision `K` is the least member of its class), whose count is Terras's density theorem and whose pointwise form is the conjecture. The direction is corrected in place and in the sixth note.

> On the tilings of order `n` the Klein four-group `<kappa, sigma>` acts; by the ordered-graph trijection `kappa` is graph complementation (the line pairing `T <-> K_P(T)`), `sigma` is the converse together with path reversal (the staircase reflection), and `kappa sigma` reverses the path arcs, `(T, P) -> (T xor E(P), P^rev)`, followed by normalization along the reversed path. Here `T xor E(P)` means reversing exactly the arcs of `P`; merely reversing the word `P` in the unchanged tournament does not give a directed Hamiltonian path. This typing correction (2026-10-25) agrees with THM-4733, Proposition A, `THM-4733-divisor-plane-of-p2qr-versus-the-marked-path-tiling-cube-3-4-3-and-4-2-4-reflections.md`. Information-flow reading, which the owner asked for: the Hamiltonian path is an integer clock, a tile `(i, j)` is a message between the times `i < j` carried forward or backward in time; `kappa` reverses every message (charge conjugation), `sigma` reverses the clock and every message (time reversal composed with conjugation), `kappa sigma` reverses only the clock. On the proper divisors `D(N)` of `N = p^2 q r` the trijection reset's `C` (complement `d -> N/d`), `Q` (exchange of the two equal-exponent primes) and `CQ` act; `C` is the arithmetic charge conjugation, `Q` the arithmetic chirality flip.
>
> Credit: Proposition K1 (i), the fixed-point-freeness of `kappa` and `kappa sigma` and the two orbit types, is THM-849 section 2 (July 2026, "what the Klein four-group really proves": for every `n >= 3`, `kappa` has no fixed tiling, nor does `kappa sigma` by the apex tile, blue orbits have size 2 and black orbits size 4), restated here and cited; the even-`k` obstruction is, concurrently and earlier by hours, the fixed-pair lemma and Theorem 1 of `divisor_reflection_resolution_20261021` (and section 1 of `reflection_context_20261020h`); their twelve-state resolution and capacity theorem are not reproduced here. Kept as this session's: the general-`N` criterion (ii)-(iii), the bijection counts and the two explicit dictionaries of K2, and the information-flow reading. mac-mini's THM-4733 (18:40, pre-audit) states the obstruction in both assignments of the two reflections (its Theorem C) and realises `D(p^2 q r)` as a fold of the four mirror-moved tiles of the order-five cube (its Theorem B); the odd-`k` embeddings and the general-`N` criterion are not there.
>

PROVED: the parity graph mod `2^k` is the `+-sqrt` graph on `F_p^*` for Fermat primes; the multiplicative Collatz `sqrt X / rad(X) X^3` is Collatz on the diagonal `m^e` and divergent off it; `chi_{-4}` is the unique level-2 provable strategy; the orbitwise two-place identity `m_L 2^(d_L)/3^L = n prod(1 + b/(3 m_l))` gives `R(d) <= n` on every `3n-1` orbit. **HYP-9160** (no slow divergence) was then PROVED by **THM-4476** (thin divergence: every non-periodic orbit has `O(X^(0.95+eps))` elements below `X`; Terras count plus a landing pigeonhole), which also recovers the in-house no-bounded-strip theorem and gives `R(d) < n` strictly; still no divergence exclusion.
>
> **Waves 16–17 (2026-09-26), summarized in section 2m.**
> * **THM-4486.** The min-max density of sign strategies is a game value. 5n+1 is settled at `2/5`, fixed by its sporadic cycle, and entropy floors are capped at `1/3`.
> * **Robin inequality.** Proved up to a polynomial factor independently by us and by codex crossroads223. It gives the sharp `L^(1/3)` constant elementarily.
> * **7n±1.** Not provable at `k <= 30`. The all-level floors for `q = 9, 11` fall just short of `log_q 2`.
>
> **Waves 14–15 (2026-09-26), summarized in section 2l.** Five new audited theorems:
> * **THM-4480.** The price of provability is the peak-discounted undecided density, with exponent `1 - H(log_q 2)` for every `q`. For `q = 3` it is `rho_L exp(-Theta(L^(1/3)))`, so P1 is negative for arbitrary edits.
> * **THM-4481.** An entropy law for sign flips: no provable `qn±1` strategy exists for `q >= 23`, and 5n+1 needs constant flip mass.
> * **THM-4482.** Ranks are tensions: Collatz's rank defect is `log(3/2)`, at `-1`.
> * **THM-4483.** Forced charges on backward trees: no finite-mass 2-adic potential rank exists, and nonnegative ones exist iff Collatz.

Scripts in `04-computation/experiments/even_graphs_20261019/`.

> ## 1. The idea in one paragraph
>
> THM-4531 reduces "natural bijection" (= S_n-equivariant, bijective on types) to Hall's condition for the stabiliser-containment graph between tournament types and even-graph types. THM-4721's Sylow refinement says the two sides have exactly as many types with a Sylow-3 symmetry, and exactly as many with a Sylow-5 symmetry. The symmetry groups of tournaments have odd order, and no odd-order group can carry both a Sylow 3- and a Sylow 5-subgroup of S_n (a 3-cycle and a 5-cycle through a common point generate A_5, A_6 or A_7: Jordan). The empty graph carries both. So the tournaments with either symmetry are one more than the even graphs they could be mapped to. This is the lane's n = 5 hand proof (Z_5 and the four Z_3's against ∅, K_{1,3}+K_1, K_{1,4}, K_{2,3}) turned into a uniform argument: "odd primes and their powers" enter exactly through the Sylow subgroups, which are the only p-subgroups for which the refined identity is proved.
>

Source: `05-knowledge/results/procgen_atlas_20260924_collatz_implication_atlas.md`, line 81. Blob: `71200857ba358d645c4a9a4b0287c416906670f3`.

> The owner asked for connections between Collatz's parts and famous problems "so that one can be shown to prove the other". Five kinds of arrows exist.
>
> 1. **Equivalence with a famous-type problem (new, Theorem M′).**
>    * Take a *supercritical adjacent block pair* `B, B'`: length `L`, weight `a`, `3^a > 2^(L+1)`, and `R_B' = R_B + 1`.
>    * Then no-divergence on `{B, B'}^N` is **equivalent** to `Z(3^a/2^L, [R_B, R_B+1]/(3^a - 2^L))`: no `xi > 0` has all of `xi (3^a/2^L)^j` in that residue interval.
>    * This is a Mahler Z-number problem of exactly the type studied by Mahler, Flatto–Lagarias–Pollington, Bugeaud, Dubickas and Akiyama–Frougny–Sakarovitch.
>    * Consequently **Collatz ⟹ T1 ⟹ these Mahler-type theorems**. This is the only "Collatz ⟹ famous-type statement" arrow found, and **PC ⟹ the same**.
>    * For general constant-weight classes only the implication `Z(3^a/2^L, I_A) ⟹ T1 on A^N` holds (Theorem M). Mahler's own `3/2` problem sits at the excluded boundary `a = L`.
> 2. **Famous conjecture ⟹ a Collatz piece (CONDITIONAL).**
>    * **Lang–Waldschmidt ⟹** the least nontrivial cycle length grows like `N^(1/2 - eps)` (Prop. LW). The computed record tracks `N^(1/2)`.
>    * **abc ⟹** slices of Rozier's LBH (Rozier 2025; Prop. R2), and **LBH ⟹ Collatz** (Rozier 2017). abc cannot reach LBH itself, because a Collatz word carries unboundedly many carry terms.
>    * **U(1/3) ⟹ HYP-9127** (in-house).

> Owner prompt: "now try EI19 and the 23-vertex fish the same way".
>
> **Method change that pays.** In a faithful realization every unit 4-cycle is a parallelogram, so edges fall into direction classes with exact integer relations from the cycle space; realizations are solutions over the free angles. A relation of support three, n_a u_a + n_b u_b + n_c u_c = 0, is a rigid triangle of unit vectors and forces a direction ratio of modulus one for EVERY realization, so a forced ratio outside a field excludes the graph from that field without any census. (Random starts over the free angles also converge almost always; the vertex-space and apex-branch searches of the first addendum were far worse.)
>
> **EI19 (HoG 51376): no faithful placement in Q(zeta55) -- PROVED.** 13 classes, 6 relations, two of support three: u5 = -(3/2)(u12 + u13) and u5 = 3(u4 + u9). Hence (v13 - v6)/(v6 - v2) = (-1 +- 2 sqrt(-2))/3 and u4/u5 = (1 +- sqrt(-35))/6 in every faithful realization; neither sqrt(-2) nor sqrt(-35) lies in Q(zeta55) (quadratic subfields Q(sqrt5), Q(sqrt-11), Q(sqrt-55); non-residue witnesses at split primes), nor in any Q(zeta_n) with 8 not dividing n. Census: 32 non-congruent real faithful realizations, all isolated, all with the forced ratios; vertex-space re-solves confirm the relation independently. The forced values are exactly the sqrt2 and sqrt35 of arXiv:2607.19995's base field Q(sqrt2, sqrt5, sqrt7), and that paper's degree-12 cap polynomial is corroborated (class-pair cosines hit its eight real roots in all 32 realizations), unlike its EI17 polynomial.
>
> **Fish (HoG 51404): OPEN, a one-parameter family.** 42 = 2*23 - 4 edges; 24 classes, 11 relations, 12 free angles, local degree of freedom 1 at every faithful sample; no support-three relation; the only support-four relation makes the spokes to vertices 0,2 and 1,3 antipodal (inscribed parallelogram = rectangle). Placement in L is then a Diophantine question about the family's curve. Scan: fixing one free direction to each of 117 L-units (110 roots of unity, five Mersenne directions, the thirds unit and conjugate) and solving the rest, LLL at 200 digits finds no further direction in L at any of the 0-10 faithful solutions per parameter. Next: the curve's genus and L-points, or an invariant forced along the whole family.
>
> **EI17 revisited.** No relation of support at most four (so no combinatorially forced angle: consistent with its hard field). Direction-space census: twelve non-congruent real faithful realizations (3,000 starts; 20,000 give the same), one more than the branch censuses; the EI17 note is upda

>         Owner prompt: spend another session looking for recursive classifications of pieces of tournament structure that can compose and grow with size in order to come to the proof of the full {7,21} spectrum.
>
> READ FIRST. The answer is one object, the graded spine grammar G(k, w): a transitive chain whose reversed arcs never cross a cut more than w deep, plus k <= 2 apexes. Each chain vertex is a letter, the Hamiltonian-path count is computed by a finite cut-state automaton (fragments of the path inside the prefix, each knowing how it is entered and which later vertices may not follow it), so H on G(k, w) is an N-rational series: pieces compose by matrix multiplication and grow by word length. The method is KNOWN (frontier dynamic programming; Codex's DEFECT_TRANSFER cutwidth transfer of 2026-10-29; and mac-mini's concurrent bounded-SPAN ribbon lane THM-4880/HYP-9473, committed 21:40-21:48, which I missed at first filing - MISTAKE-720 - and which reaches 100,000 for span 2 except 7, 21, 47 and 5,000 for span 3 except 7, 21; span s implies cutwidth <= 2s - 1). The repository's spectrum families are the smallest grades: G(1,0) = one-apex, G(2,0) = two-apex (containing THM-4875's family F1, which alone has positive miss density), connected G(0,1) = upset tournaments (Moon's interval chains; the all-spans-1 chain is the tribonacci tower, THM-4865/THM-4731 (6)), fa <= 2 (THM-4748) inside G(0,2).
>
> THE FINDING (pre-registered the other way, commit 756414ff80). The apex-free cutwidth-2 grammar G(0,2) attains EVERY odd number <= 8191 except 7 and 21 (4094 of 4096), with at most five reversed arcs per value; below 512 the exact minimal-arc layers are 1, 8, 75, 133, 36, 1 values with 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 arcs, so the two-arc layer (THM-4748) plus the three-arc layer (15 pairwise-configuration types) already covers 217 of the 256 odd numbers below 512. I had predicted 10-60 misses below 300. G(1,1) and G(0,3) are also complete below 512; G(2,0) reproduces the two-apex witnesses below 2048. A transitive order with reversed arcs never more than two deep is therefore the most structured complete candidate in the repository, and HYP-9470 (C) conjectures it is onto (which would prove Chan-Pak). A counting lemma (PROVED) says no family with a bounded number of reversed arcs can be complete, so the proof must be a numeration theorem in which the arcs are digits; the two-radix heuristic (powers of 2 from single runs, powers of 3 from two interleaved runs) is consistent with every grade computed: binary-only grades (one-apex: exactly half of the odd numbers below 2^21; interva

One real result: HYP-9668 (E') is false (the image of d - dW ^ for Broughton's polynomial x + x^2 y, and for every x^(l+1) y + r(x), is not a Mathieu-Zhao subspace although it has codimension one and 1 can be outside it). The tournament half is, after the audit, a credited re-derivation of the O/Z/J root-change grammar plus two finite measurements of its sub-languages. JC(2) and Chan-Pak remain OPEN.
>
> AUDIT: independent, own code, PASS WITH CORRECTIONS (five major findings, sixteen minor, eight upgrades; all applied; MISTAKE-768). Packet 05-knowledge/results/kps_s25_euler_field_bridge_law_20261111/ (README.md, PREREGISTRATION.md, scripts/, runs/, audit/).
>
> PART A (THM-5147; PROVED)
> - Lemma: Im(d/dx - W_x) + Im(d/dy - W_y) = {div(v) - v(W)}. If xi(W) = W and div xi = delta is constant, W h = (xi + delta) h modulo the image; if eta(W) = 0 and div eta = 0, eta(h) lies in the image.
> - W = x^(l+1) y + r(x), r(0) = 0: the image is the kernel of lambda_r(x^k y^j) = j! [x^((l+1)(j+1)-1-k)] e^(-r), has codimension one, contains W^m for m >= l and never x^l W^n = n! x^l. Not Mathieu-Zhao; 1 lies in the image iff [x^l] e^(-r) = 0. For r = x the author's proof uses the Euler field x d/dx - l y d/dy (divergence 1 - l); the general case and the elementary proof of codimension one are the audit's.
> - Euler obstruction: an Euler field with divergence a non-positive integer plus one eigen-polynomial of nonzero class excludes the Mathieu property. For xy and for (f - a)(g - b) of a Keller pair the divergence is 1.
> - Radial identity for Stein modules: [(UV)^m h]_z = [(E + 1)...(E + m) h]_z, E = (U d_u + V d_v)/2.
> - The general Image Conjecture is already REFUTED in canon (THM-2801); this is one more failure, in the smallest setting, not a first counterexample. HYP-9668 (E') REFUTED; (Q) and (D') OPEN.
